# The rebalance survey: every unit measured against the game's own prices

Generated by `tools/rebalance_survey.py` (model: `unit_model.py`) from the vanilla database. 1100 recruitable units, 1100 with a multiplayer price. See `docs/METHOD.md` for the method.

## The valuation

Composition weights, chosen by coordinate descent to make the prices most consistent:

| weight | value | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| ranged | 1.00 | a point of missile dps, against a point of melee dps |
| impact | 0.50 | a point of collision dps |
| ability | 0.00 | a point of breath-attack or bombardment dps |
| missile_tough | 0.30 | share of toughness that is toughness against missiles |
| range_exp | 0.50 | missile dps × (range / 150 m) ^ range_exp |
| window | 240 s | shooting time a battle affords; ammunition beyond it is worth nothing |
| reach | 2.0 | ranks of attackers that reach the exposed edge of a regiment (engagement geometry) |

Price regression, log(multiplayer cost) on the measurements, one price line per caste (robust fit, 1100 units). Residual spread: robust σ 0.166 (a typical unit is priced within ±18% of its caste's line), rms 0.200, 5-fold cross-validated rms 0.208.

| caste | price ∝ regiment power ^ | at power 1.00 (one Empire Knights regiment) | pooled line says |
|---|---|---|---|
| melee infantry | 0.78 | 510 gold (typical size 99) | 597 gold, ^ 0.60 |
| missile infantry | 0.57 | 552 gold (typical size 68) | 626 gold, ^ 0.60 |
| monstrous infantry | 0.55 | 677 gold (typical size 17) | 741 gold, ^ 0.60 |
| melee cavalry | 0.71 | 754 gold (typical size 53) | 645 gold, ^ 0.60 |
| missile cavalry | 0.55 | 695 gold (typical size 57) | 640 gold, ^ 0.60 |
| monstrous cavalry | 0.64 | 745 gold (typical size 19) | 731 gold, ^ 0.60 |
| war beast | 0.38 | 558 gold (typical size 32) | 687 gold, ^ 0.60 |
| monster | 0.37 | 922 gold (typical size 1) | 1041 gold, ^ 0.60 |
| chariot | 0.41 | 772 gold (typical size 3) | 934 gold, ^ 0.60 |
| warmachine | 0.29 | 972 gold (typical size 3) | 925 gold, ^ 0.60 |

The pooled column is one line through every caste: where it disagrees with the caste's own line, CA prices that caste differently from what the square-law model says it is worth (flatter for monsters, artillery and chariots: the top of those castes is cheap for its measured power, the bottom dear). The rebalance judges each unit against its own caste's line, so a monster is compared with monsters; the pooled line is the cross-caste question for the lore layer.

Trait premiums (shared by every caste):

| term | coefficient | read as |
|---|---|---|
| log entities | -0.075 | at equal regiment power, price ∝ entities ^ -0.08 (negative: concentration is worth more) |
| flying | -0.002 | -0% |
| causes fear | +0.046 | +5% |
| causes terror | +0.112 | +12% |
| unbreakable | +0.046 | +5% |
| immune to psychology | +0.044 | +5% |
| regeneration | -0.003 | -0% |
| stalk | +0.189 | +21% |
| vanguard | -0.085 | -8% |
| single | +0.101 | +11% |
| renown | +0.131 | +14% |
| log speed | 0.131 | price ∝ speed ^ 0.13 |
| active abilities (each, up to 3) | +0.067 | +7% per ability |
| contact | -0.003 | -0% |
| undead | +0.008 | +1% |
| expendable | -0.231 | -21% |

## Every unit, by faction

**reg** is the regiment's power (the Empire Knights regiment = 1.00), **power** per model on the same scale (reg / entities × 60, so a 60-model unit of Empire Knight quality reads 1.00). **fair** is the price the valuation gives; **residual** is log(price / fair): + over-priced, − bargain. **reg@price** is the regiment power at which the unit would be worth its current price, the target the price-consistency rebalance uses.
